{"method":"get_file","path":"templates/app/ShareFlow.jsx","content":"// DataQ — Share Suite drawer. Invite people to a suite by email with a\n// view/edit permission, and manage who already has access. Built on the\n// design-system Drawer primitive. Exports window.ShareSuiteDrawer.\nconst DQshare = window.DataQDesignSystem_317fec;\n\nconst INITIAL_SHARES = [\n  { name: 'Jane Doe', email: 'jane.doe@acme.com', perm: 'owner' },\n  { name: 'Arijit Sen', email: 'arijit@acme.com', perm: 'edit' },\n  { name: 'Mei Lin', email: 'mei.lin@acme.com', perm: 'view' },\n];\n\nfunction PermSelect({ value, onChange, disabled }) {\n  return (\n    <span style={{ position: 'relative', display: 'inline-block' }}>\n      <select value={value} onChange={(e) => onChange(e.target.value)} disabled={disabled}\n        style={{\n          appearance: 'none', WebkitAppearance: 'none',\n          height: 30, padding: '0 26px 0 10px', boxSizing: 'border-box',\n          background: 'var(--bg-card)', border: '1px solid var(--border-input)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)',\n          fontFamily: 'var(--font-sans)', fontSize: 13, fontWeight: 500,\n          color: disabled ? 'var(--text-muted)' : 'var(--text-primary)',\n          cursor: disabled ? 'default' : 'pointer',\n        }}>\n        {disabled\n          ? <option value=\"owner\">Owner</option>\n          : <React.Fragment><option value=\"view\">Can view</option><option value=\"edit\">Can edit</option><option value=\"remove\">Remove</option></React.Fragment>}\n      </select>\n      {!disabled && (\n        <span style={{ position: 'absolute', right: 9, top: '50%', transform: 'translateY(-50%)', pointerEvents: 'none', color: 'var(--text-muted)' }}>\n          <window.Icon.down size={10} />\n        </span>\n      )}\n    </span>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction ShareSuiteDrawer({ open, onClose, suiteName }) {\n  const { Drawer, Button, Avatar } = DQshare;\n  if (!Drawer) return null;\n  const [email, setEmail] = React.useState('');\n  const [perm, setPerm] = React.useState('view');\n  const [shares, setShares] = React.useState(INITIAL_SHARES);\n  React.useEffect(() => { if (open) { setEmail(''); setPerm('view'); setShares(INITIAL_SHARES); } }, [open]);\n\n  const addShare = () => {\n    const v = email.trim();\n    if (!v) return;\n    const name = v.split('@')[0].split(/[.\\-_]/).map((p) => p.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + p.slice(1)).join(' ');\n    setShares((prev) => [...prev, { name, email: v, perm }]);\n    setEmail('');\n    setPerm('view');\n  };\n\n  const changePerm = (idx, value) => {\n    if (value === 'remove') { setShares((prev) => prev.filter((_, i) => i !== idx)); return; }\n    setShares((prev) => prev.map((s, i) => (i === idx ? { ...s, perm: value } : s)));\n  };\n\n  const footer = (\n    <React.Fragment>\n      <Button variant=\"secondary\" onClick={onClose}>Done</Button>\n      <Button variant=\"primary\" iconLeft={<window.Icon.share size={14} />} onClick={onClose}>Share</Button>\n    </React.Fragment>\n  );\n\n  return (\n    <Drawer open={open} onClose={onClose} width={500}\n      title=\"Share suite\" subtitle={suiteName} footer={footer}>\n      <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: 20 }}>\n        {/* Suite name banner */}\n        <div style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 12, padding: '12px 14px', background: 'var(--bg-subtle)', border: '1px solid var(--border)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)' }}>\n          <div style={{ width: 34, height: 34, borderRadius: 8, flex: 'none', background: 'var(--ink)', color: 'var(--indigo-300)', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center' }}>{React.createElement(window.Icon.suites, { size: 18 })}</div>\n          <div style={{ minWidth: 0 }}>\n            <div style={{ fontSize: 14, fontWeight: 600, color: 'var(--text-primary)' }}>{suiteName}</div>\n            <div style={{ fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--text-muted)' }}>{shares.length} {shares.length === 1 ? 'person has' : 'people have'} access</div>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n\n        {/* Share with */}\n        <div>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 13, fontWeight: 600, color: 'var(--text-primary)', marginBottom: 8 }}>Share with</div>\n          <div style={{ display: 'flex', gap: 8, alignItems: 'stretch' }}>\n            <span style={{ flex: 1, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 8, height: 40, padding: '0 12px', background: 'var(--bg-card)', border: '1px solid var(--border-input)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)' }}>\n              <window.Icon.user size={15} style={{ color: 'var(--text-muted)', flex: 'none' }} />\n              <input value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} placeholder=\"name@acme.com\"\n                onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === 'Enter') addShare(); }}\n                style={{ flex: 1, border: 'none', outline: 'none', background: 'transparent', fontFamily: 'var(--font-sans)', fontSize: 14, color: 'var(--text-primary)', minWidth: 0 }} />\n            </span>\n            <PermSelect value={perm} onChange={setPerm} />\n          </div>\n          <div style={{ marginTop: 8 }}>\n            <Button variant=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\" iconLeft={<window.Icon.plus size={13} />} onClick={addShare}>Add person</Button>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n\n        {/* People with access */}\n        <div>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 12, fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '.04em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--text-muted)', marginBottom: 10 }}>People with access</div>\n          <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: 4 }}>\n            {shares.map((sh, i) => (\n              <div key={sh.email} style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 12, padding: '8px 0' }}>\n                <Avatar name={sh.name} size={34} tone={sh.perm === 'owner' ? 'indigo' : 'gray'} />\n                <div style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 0 }}>\n                  <div style={{ fontSize: 14, fontWeight: 600, color: 'var(--text-primary)' }}>{sh.name}{sh.perm === 'owner' && <span style={{ fontWeight: 500, color: 'var(--text-muted)' }}> (you)</span>}</div>\n                  <div style={{ fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--text-muted)', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }}>{sh.email}</div>\n                </div>\n                <PermSelect value={sh.perm} disabled={sh.perm === 'owner'} onChange={(v) => changePerm(i, v)} />\n              </div>\n            ))}\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </Drawer>\n  );\n}\n\nObject.assign(window, { ShareSuiteDrawer });\n","contentType":"application/javascript","isBase64":false,"truncated":false}

{"method":"get_file","path":"templates/error/index.html","content":"<!-- @template name=\"DataQ Error Pages\" description=\"Standard 4xx & 5xx HTTP error pages in the app shell — switchable across 404/403/401/429/500/503/504 with recovery actions and a request id.\" -->\n<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<title>DataQ — Error</title>\n<style>\n  * { box-sizing: border-box; }\n  html, body { margin: 0; height: 100%; font-family: var(--font-sans); color: var(--text-body); background: var(--bg-app); }\n  #root { height: 100%; }\n</style>\n<script src=\"https://unpkg.com/react@18.3.1/umd/react.development.js\" integrity=\"sha384-hD6/REDACTED/6onKUg4lLsHAs9EBPT82L\" crossorigin=\"anonymous\"></script>\n<script src=\"https://unpkg.com/react-dom@18.3.1/umd/react-dom.development.js\" integrity=\"REDACTED\" crossorigin=\"anonymous\"></script>\n<script src=\"https://unpkg.com/@babel/standalone@7.29.0/babel.min.js\" integrity=\"sha384-m08KidiNqLdpJqLq95G/LEi8Qvjl/REDACTED\" crossorigin=\"anonymous\"></script>\n<script src=\"./ds-base.js\"></script>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div id=\"root\"></div>\n<script type=\"text/babel\" data-presets=\"react\">\n  const { ErrorState, SegmentedControl, Tag } = window.DataQDesignSystem_317fec;\n\n  // The standard DataQ error catalog — 4xx (client) and 5xx (server).\n  const ERRORS = {\n    '400': { tone: 'client', title: 'Bad request', message: 'The request was malformed — a parameter or filter is invalid. Adjust your input and try again.' },\n    '401': { tone: 'client', title: 'Session expired', message: 'Your Azure AD session has ended. Sign in again to continue monitoring your data.', primary: { label: 'Sign in', href: '../app/index.html' } },\n    '403': { tone: 'client', title: 'Access denied', message: \"You don't have permission to view this suite or workspace. Ask an admin to grant access via RBAC.\" },\n    '404': { tone: 'client', title: 'Page not found', message: \"The suite, connection, or page you're looking for doesn't exist or has moved.\" },\n    '429': { tone: 'client', title: 'Too many requests', message: \"You've hit the rate limit. Pause for a moment before retrying — scheduled runs are unaffected.\" },\n    '500': { tone: 'server', title: 'Something went wrong on our end', message: 'DataQ hit an unexpected error processing your request. The engineering team has been notified automatically.' },\n    '502': { tone: 'server', title: 'Bad gateway', message: 'An upstream service returned an invalid response. This is usually transient — retry in a few seconds.' },\n    '503': { tone: 'server', title: 'Service unavailable', message: 'DataQ is temporarily down for maintenance or under heavy load. Monitoring continues; the console will return shortly.' },\n    '504': { tone: 'server', title: 'Gateway timeout', message: 'A datasource or orchestration provider took too long to respond. Check the connection, then retry.' },\n  };\n\n  // Minimal shell chrome (header + watermark) so the error sits in-product.\n  function Shell({ children }) {\n    return (\n      <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', height: '100vh', background: 'var(--bg-app)' }}>\n        <header style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 10, padding: '0 24px', height: 56, flex: 'none', background: 'var(--bg-card)', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--border)' }}>\n          <a href=\"../app/index.html\" style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 10, textDecoration: 'none' }}>\n            <img src=\"../../assets/logo/dataq-mark.svg\" width=\"28\" height=\"28\" alt=\"\" />\n            <span style={{ fontWeight: 700, fontSize: 17, color: 'var(--ink)' }}>DataQ</span>\n          </a>\n        </header>\n        <main style={{ flex: 1, position: 'relative', overflow: 'hidden', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center' }}>\n          <img src=\"../../assets/logo/dataq-mark.svg\" aria-hidden=\"true\" style={{ position: 'absolute', right: -90, bottom: -90, width: 360, opacity: 0.05 }} />\n          <div style={{ position: 'relative', width: '100%' }}>{children}</div>\n        </main>\n      </div>\n    );\n  }\n\n  function ErrorPage() {\n    const [code, setCode] = React.useState('404');\n    const e = ERRORS[code];\n    const codes = Object.keys(ERRORS);\n\n    const primary = e.primary || (e.tone === 'server'\n      ? { label: 'Retry', onClick: () => setCode(code) }\n      : { label: 'Back to Results', href: '../app/index.html' });\n    const secondary = e.tone === 'server'\n      ? { label: 'View status page', href: '#' }\n      : { label: 'Go to Connections', href: '../app/index.html' };\n\n    return (\n      <Shell>\n        {/* demo switcher — not part of the shipped page */}\n        <div style={{ position: 'absolute', top: 16, left: '50%', transform: 'translateX(-50%)', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'center', gap: 8, zIndex: 5 }}>\n          <span style={{ fontSize: 11, fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '.06em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--text-muted)' }}>Status code · demo</span>\n          <SegmentedControl size=\"sm\" value={code} onChange={setCode}\n            options={codes.map((c) => ({ value: c, label: c }))} />\n        </div>\n\n        <ErrorState\n          code={code}\n          tone={e.tone}\n          title={e.title}\n          message={e.message}\n          primaryAction={primary}\n          secondaryAction={secondary}\n          requestId={e.tone === 'server' ? 'req_9f2a1c7b3e' : null}\n        />\n      </Shell>\n    );\n  }\n\n  ReactDOM.createRoot(document.getElementById('root')).render(<ErrorPage />);\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n","contentType":"text/html","isBase64":false,"truncated":false}

I've now pulled and read **every screen** in the prototype (`templates/app/*` + `templates/error/`) plus the canonical `readme.md`, and diffed them against our shipped frontend. Here's the full-scope picture — you're right that the 23-task tracking under-counted it.

## The complete prototype screen inventory vs. where we stand

The prototype's `index.html` registers **13 screens** behind a single shell. Mapping each to our codebase:

| # | Prototype screen | Route | Our status | Action for W6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **AppShell** (header + sider + footer nav + watermark) | — | [App.tsx](frontend/src/App.tsx) shell exists, but nav = Connections·Suites·Results·Profile | **Restructure**: add Dashboard, footer group (Admin·Settings·Documentation), `/`→`/dashboard` |
| 2 | **DashboardScreen** (KPIs · Quality Trends · Suite Performance · Recent Runs) | `/dashboard` | ❌ **doesn't exist** | **New** — landing page; chart-gated |
| 3 | ConnectionsScreen (card grid) | `/connections` | ✅ [Connections.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Connections.tsx) | Align only |
| 4 | **ConnectionFlow** — Source-select → Config (2 pages) | `/connections/new` | 🟡 [ConnectionNew.tsx](frontend/src/pages/ConnectionNew.tsx) is one page | Verify/adopt the categorized **source-select** step (Orchestration first) |
| 5 | SuitesScreen (two-panel) | `/suites` | ✅ [Suites.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Suites.tsx) | Align only |
| 6 | **SuiteFlow** (New Suite) | `/suites/new` | ❌ create is a **drawer** (`SuiteDrawer`) | **Restructure** drawer → page |
| 7 | CheckFlow (Add Check) | `/suites/:id/checks/new` | ✅ [CheckNew.tsx](frontend/src/pages/CheckNew.tsx) | Align only |
| 8 | **ResultsScreen + RunDetailScreen** | `/results` + `/results/:runId` | 🟡 Results shipped; run-detail is a **drawer** ([Results.tsx:54](frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx#L54)) | **Restructure** drawer → route; add KPI cards + CSV/JSON download |
| 9 | **ProfileScreen** (identity + alert channels) | `/profile` | ❌ routes to placeholder `Home` ([App.tsx:100](frontend/src/App.tsx#L100)) | **New** real content |
| 10 | AdminScreen (Control Center) | `/admin` | ✅ [Admin.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Admin.tsx) (#289) | Reconcile layout (MetricCards + Members&access + All suites) |
| 11 | **SettingsScreen** (General·Secrets·Notifications·Danger) | `/settings` | ❌ **doesn't exist** | **New** (was Week-7, backend-gated) |
| 12 | ShareFlow (Share drawer) | drawer | ✅ [SharePanel.tsx](frontend/src/components/suites/SharePanel.tsx) | Stays a drawer ✓ (the *only* drawer) |
| 13 | **Error pages** (400–504 via `ErrorState`) | catch-all | 🟡 [ErrorBoundary.tsx](frontend/src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx)/[Forbidden.tsx](frontend/src/components/Forbidden.tsx) partial | **New** full 4xx/5xx catalog |

### What the docs under-scoped (the gap you flagged)

The Week-6 list (`progress.md` 23 tasks) tracks the Dashboard, New-Suite page, edit-flows, results route, and error pages — but **three real screens aren't in it**:
- **Profile** — still a placeholder (`Home`), but the prototype has a full identity + alert-channels screen
- **Settings** — entirely new (General / Secrets / Notifications / Danger zone); was punted to Week 7 as "backend-gated"
- **Connection source-select split** — the prototype's two-page New-Connection flow isn't explicitly a task

So full Week-6 scope = the 23 tracked tasks **+** Profile content, Settings page, Admin-layout reconcile, and the connection source/config split.

### Out of scope (correctly)
Marketing landing page (post-v1), the **"View as" role switch** (prototype demo-only, not adopted), dark mode (post-v1, but the token contract is already dark-ready).

## The one structural decision the prototype is silent on

The whole `/dashboard` + Quality-Trends + per-check-trend work **gates on a chart library** — antd ships none, and `recharts` vs `@ant-design/charts` has real bundle-size + `pnpm audit` consequences. That's the ADR. Two calls to make before I draft it:

README.md
---- 0021 for format ----
# ADR 0021 — Live test/demo-data environment (retail model) lives outside the repo

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date:** 2026-06-21
- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
- **Related:** ADR [0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) (orchestration abstraction — the flows exercise it), [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) / [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) (Azure/Key Vault is one impl behind each seam; no Azure resource names in the repo), [0003](0003-gx-only-for-v1.md) (GX run paths the flows feed). Discharges the standing **"live warehouse/file run — deferred Week-1 smoke"** caveat carried in CLAUDE.md §13 and `docs/progress.md`.

## Context

Every DataQ run path — Snowflake (`SnowflakeCheckRunner`), flat-file (`FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution), Unity Catalog (`UnityCatalogCheckRunner`) — is unit-tested against canned in-memory DataFrames with the **network seam stubbed** (real GX execution, mocked IO). That is deliberate and correct for CI, but it means no run has ever touched a live warehouse, live ADLS/S3 object, or live UC table. The roadmap has carried this as the *"deferred Week-1 smoke"* since Week 1.

Closing it needs a **realistic, repeatable data environment**: real datasources with believable data, real orchestration picking the data up, and the three DataQ run paths executing against it end-to-end. The data should model a real domain (retail) so checks, profiling, severity tiers and trends look like production, not toy fixtures.

The question this ADR settles is **what, if anything, of that environment belongs in the DataQ source repo.**

## Decision

**The test/demo-data environment is a harness that lives _outside_ the DataQ source repo.** None of the setup code is committed:

- **Terraform** that provisions Azure + Snowflake infra once accounts are enabled — local / separate, **not git-tracked** (also keeps Azure resource names out of the repo, per ADR 0010/0013).
- **Mock-data generators** for the retail model and the seed data they produce — out of repo.
- **The Databricks RAW→Silver→Gold notebook** that processes landed files — out of repo.

What **is** git-tracked is only documentation that points at the harness and records this decision: this ADR, plus the roadmap / `progress.md` pointers that finally give the deferred-smoke caveat an owner.

**Retail data model** the harness seeds (domains → entities): Product (style, colour, material, SKU) · Inventory (on-hand, planned) · Location (store, address, store hours) · Sales (placed, shipped, audited) · Return/Cancellation (return, cancellation, guest, platform) · Logistics (shipment, tracking, carrier) · Guest/Customer (details, survey, chatbot feedback). Plus cross-cutting: on-sale/discount, promotion/award/coupon, tax rate, exchange rate, product price, misc.

Data is split across **ADLS · Snowflake · Unity Catalog**; orchestration across **ADF · Airflow** (both behind the existing `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction — ADR 0004; no provider-specific branching).

**Three reference flows** the harness drives, each exercising a distinct DataQ run path:

- **Flow A — load to warehouse.** File lands in ADLS → ADF/Airflow picks it up → loads into Snowflake / UC → DataQ runs a suite against the warehouse table (`SnowflakeCheckRunner` / `UnityCatalogCheckRunner`).
- **Flow B — medallion.** File lands in UC RAW → Databricks notebook runs → processes RAW → Silver → Gold → DataQ runs suites against the UC tables.
- **Flow C — flat-file in place.** File lands in ADLS and **stays a flat file** (no warehouse load) → DataQ runs a suite directly against it (`FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution picks the latest batch).

**Hosting — local/fast-iteration profile (chosen).** Airflow runs **locally via the official `apache/airflow` docker-compose** (webserver + scheduler + Postgres metadata DB + optional Celery workers), while **ADLS / Snowflake / Unity Catalog point at real cloud accounts**. This keeps the orchestration loop cheap and fast to iterate while the datasources stay realistic. Terraform is **not** used for the local Airflow (docker-compose is the norm; the `kreuzwerker/docker` provider is unnecessary friction); ADF, being managed, is exercised in its cloud form. A fully-cloud profile (Terraform-provisioned **Azure Managed Airflow** or AKS-hosted, alongside the rest of the infra) is a valid alternative for a more production-like run, but is **not** the v1 default. Either way Airflow stays an `OrchestrationProvider` behind the abstraction (ADR 0004) — DataQ is agnostic to how it is hosted.

## Consequences

**Positive**
- The deferred live-warehouse/file smoke gets a concrete, owned mechanism instead of floating as a caveat.
- The repo stays clean of infra/seed/harness code and Azure-specific resource names (ADR 0010/0013 anti-lock-in holds).
- The three flows give real end-to-end coverage of all three run paths **and** the ADF + Airflow orchestration trigger-on-success path (ADR 0004), against believable retail data.
- Profiler, severity tiers, and Week-6 trends/anomaly baselines get realistic data to render against.

**Negative / watch**
- The harness is unversioned alongside the product; its setup steps must be documented well enough to reproduce (README in the external harness location).
- **Boundary to hold:** if any harness artifact later becomes a *generic, user-facing reference template* (the way `integrations/airflow/dataq_airflow_callback.py` is a shipped template, **not** app code), it would move into `integrations/` (e.g. `integrations/databricks/`) and become git-tracked — but the retail-specific harness stays out. This ADR governs the demo harness, not future generic templates.

## Alternatives considered

- **Commit the harness into the repo (e.g. `scripts/mockdata/`, `infra/terraform/`).** Rejected: bloats the product repo with environment-specific seed + Azure resource names, working against the anti-lock-in posture (ADR 0010/0013), and couples product CI to harness churn. Credentials-in-tracked-files risk too (working agreement).
- **Keep relying on canned-frame unit tests only.** Rejected: never discharges the live smoke; profiling/trends keep running on toy data.
- **Ship the Databricks notebook as an `integrations/` template now.** Deferred: it's currently the retail-specific harness, not a generalised user template. Revisit if/when a generic medallion-integration reference is wanted.
```

1	# Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)
2	
3	Each ADR captures a single significant architecture decision: the context, the decision, the consequences, and the alternatives considered. New ADRs are append-only — supersede an old decision by adding a new ADR and marking the old one's status as `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`.
4	
5	## Format
6	
7	- File name: `NNNN-short-kebab-slug.md` (zero-padded 4-digit sequence)
8	- Frontmatter fields: Status, Date, Deciders
9	- Sections: Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered, Related (optional)
10	- Keep each ADR short — 1–2 pages. If it grows past that, the decision is probably two decisions.
11	
12	## Index
13	
14	| # | Title | Status |
15	|---|---|---|
16	| [0001](0001-trunk-based-branching.md) | Trunk-based branching with squash-merge into `main` | Accepted |
17	| [0002](0002-conventional-commits.md) | Conventional commits for PR titles and commit messages | Accepted |
18	| [0003](0003-gx-only-for-v1.md) | GX-only for v1; DQX deferred to v1.1 for DLT/streaming | Accepted |
19	| [0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) | Unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction for ADF and Airflow | Accepted |
20	| [0005](0005-severity-tier-weights.md) | Severity tier weights (warn / fail / critical → health score) | Accepted |
21	| [0006](0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md) | ADF webhook authentication (shared secret in URL + hard-cutover rotation) | Accepted |
22	| [0007](0007-airflow-callback-model.md) | Airflow callback model (HMAC-signed webhook + polling fallback) | Accepted |
23	| [0009](0009-flat-monorepo-layout.md) | Repo layout — flat monorepo (`backend/` + `frontend/`) | Accepted |
24	| [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) | Provider-agnostic infrastructure seams (Azure is the default, not the architecture) | Accepted |
25	| [0011](0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md) | Extensibility seams for deferred connectors and integrations | Accepted |
26	| [0012](0012-monitor-kind-seam.md) | Monitor-kind seam (`check.kind` discriminator + numeric metric storage) | Accepted |
27	| [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) | Marketplace distribution (customer-deployed BYOL) and anti-vendor-lock-in guardrails | Accepted |
28	| [0014](0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md) | Cross-dataset reconciliation as a `comparison` check kind (reuse FastAPI_DataComparison engine) | Accepted |
29	| [0016](0016-severity-derivation-semantics.md) | Severity derivation semantics (band the unexpected-%, thresholds override GX success) | Accepted |
30	| [0017](0017-python-313-runtime-upgrade.md) | Upgrade Python runtime 3.11 → 3.13 (3.14 deferred — GX-capped); bundled with the Snowflake 3→4 CVE refresh | Accepted |
31	| [0018](0018-results-surface-and-grafana-deferral.md) | Results surface is an in-app page (suite-scoped authz + PII redaction); Grafana deferred to optional ops add-on | Accepted |
32	| [0019](0019-custom-sql-check-kind.md) | Custom-SQL checks ride `kind='expectation'` via GX `UnexpectedRowsExpectation` (no new kind); read-only validation + SQL-datasource gating | Accepted |
33	| [0020](0020-history-and-audit-strategy.md) | History/audit: per-entity Type-4 snapshot tables (`check_versions`, `connection_versions`) where config history is needed; no SCD-2; credentials never snapshotted; cascade-delete accepted; cross-entity audit log deferred | Accepted |
34	| [0021](0021-demo-test-data-environment-strategy.md) | Live test/demo-data environment (retail model, 3 reference flows) lives outside the repo — Terraform/mock-data/Databricks notebook not git-tracked; discharges the deferred live-warehouse/file smoke | Accepted |

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/docs/adr/0022-week6-prototype-adoption-and-chart-library.md",
  "content": "# ADR 0022 — Week-6 prototype adoption (dedicated-page flows + full screen set) and chart library (recharts)\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2026-06-21\n- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771\n- **Related:** ADR [0018](0018-results-surface-and-grafana-deferral.md) (results is an in-app page, not Grafana — this ADR builds its dashboard/charts), [0005](0005-severity-tier-weights.md) / [0016](0016-severity-derivation-semantics.md) (the severity/status model the screens render), [0011](0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md) (the `ResultPublisher` alerting seam Profile/Settings notification UI fronts), [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) / [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) (no Azure resource names baked into the new Settings/Secrets screen — Key Vault is one impl behind the seam). Tracked in epic [#175](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/175).\n\n## Context\n\nWeek 6 (\"Results dashboard + alerting\") is also the week we adopt the **DataQ Design System** prototype (claude.ai/design project *\"DataQ Design System\"*, `projectId 317fec67-2b8a-498c-8f6b-523750916a8d`; its `readme.md` is the spec). The prototype was reverse-engineered from the shipped indigo/antd app, so the **foundation already matches** (`theme.ts` palette, `resultsFormat.ts` status model, the 56px-header/220px-sider shell) — this is **not a re-skin**. It adopts the prototype's **structure**.\n\nTwo problems pushed this to an ADR:\n\n1. **The Week-6 task list under-scoped the work.** `progress.md` tracked 23 tasks but the prototype's `templates/app/index.html` registers **13 screens**, and three real screens fell out of the tracking: **Profile** (still a placeholder `Home` component), **Settings** (didn't exist; had been punted to Week 7 as \"backend-gated\"), and the **New-Connection source-select split**. The decision now is to build the **full prototype screen set in Week 6**, not a subset.\n2. **The dashboard + trend charts gate on a charting library** the prototype is silent about (it fakes charts with divs). antd ships none, so this is a real dependency decision with bundle-size + `pnpm audit` consequences.\n\n## Decision\n\n### A. Adopt the full prototype screen set in Week 6\n\nEvery create/edit flow becomes a **dedicated, deep-linkable page**; **the Share drawer is the _only_ surviving drawer**. Where the prototype differs from the shipped app, **the prototype wins**. The 13 screens and their disposition:\n\n| Screen | Route | Disposition |\n|---|---|---|\n| AppShell / nav | — | Restructure: sider → **Dashboard · Connections · Suites · Results · Profile**; footer group **Admin · Settings · Documentation**; login redirect `/` → `/dashboard` |\n| **Dashboard** | `/dashboard` | **New** landing — KPI cards, Quality Trends chart, Suite Performance bars, Recent Runs table (gated on §B) |\n| Connections | `/connections` | Exists — align |\n| New Connection | `/connections/new` → source-select → config | Adopt the **categorized source-select** step (Orchestration first), then spec-driven config |\n| Suites | `/suites` | Exists — align (two-panel) |\n| **New Suite** | `/suites/new` | **Restructure** `SuiteDrawer` create → page |\n| Add Check | `/suites/:id/checks/new` | Exists — align |\n| **Edit flows** | `/connections/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/checks/:checkId/edit` | **Restructure** the Connection/Suite/Check **edit drawers** → pages reusing the create page + prefill |\n| Results | `/results` | Exists — add KPI cards + filter expansion |\n| **Run detail** | `/results/:runId` | **Restructure** the in-page `openRun` drawer → routed page; add CSV/JSON download |\n| **Profile** | `/profile` | **New** real content (identity + alert-channel toggles) — replaces the placeholder `Home` |\n| Admin | `/admin` | Exists (#289) — reconcile layout to the prototype (MetricCards + Members & access + All suites) |\n| **Settings** | `/settings` | **New** — General · Secrets (Key Vault) · Notifications · Danger zone. **Pulled into Week 6** (was Week 7) |\n| Share | drawer | Stays a drawer (the only one) ✓ |\n| **Error pages** | router catch-all + `ErrorBoundary` fallback | **New** `ErrorState`-based 400/401/403/404/429/500/502/503/504 catalog |\n\n**Built with real Ant Design**, reading tokens from `theme.ts`. The prototype's framework-free `.jsx` recreations and `_ds_bundle.js` are **reference only** (per the design `readme.md`).\n\n**Honesty constraints carried forward:**\n- Render **only backend-backed KPIs** on the Dashboard/Results (no invented metrics — the W7 \"KPI honesty pass\"). Where the prototype shows a metric with no backend (e.g. \"Avg. Time to Resolution\"), either wire it or omit it — don't fake it.\n- Failing-row **sample drill-down stays withheld** pending row-level PII redaction ([#226](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/226), ADR 0018).\n- Settings **Secrets** tab shows Key Vault state via the generic secret seam — **no hardcoded Azure resource names** in component/business logic (ADR 0010/0013).\n- Notification toggles (Profile/Settings) front the **`ResultPublisher`** seam (ADR 0011); Teams is the v1 impl.\n\n### B. Chart library: **recharts**\n\nAdopt **recharts** for the Dashboard KPI/trend charts and the per-check historical trend. Rationale: lightweight composable React-native SVG (~95 KB gz), MIT, clean audit history, and **lazy-loaded** so it ships only on chart-bearing routes (`/dashboard`, run/check detail) — keeping the initial bundle lean, consistent with the existing code-split routes in `App.tsx`. It clears the `pnpm audit --audit-level=high` + bundle-size merge gate.\n\n### Explicitly NOT adopted\n- **\"View as\" role switch** (prototype user-menu demo only) — real authz is server-driven via `/me` (#289).\n- **Marketing landing page** (`templates/marketing/`) — post-v1.\n- **Dark mode** — post-v1; the semantic-token contract already keeps the app dark-ready (`tokens/dark.css`), so no work now.\n- The prototype `_ds_bundle.js` / framework-free component recreations — reference only.\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive**\n- One honest, complete Week-6 scope: every shipped screen has a prototype-matched target, and the three under-tracked screens (Profile, Settings, connection source-select) are now first-class.\n- Deep-linkable, refreshable pages for every flow (drawers were not) — better URLs, back-nav, and browser history. Only Share stays modal.\n- recharts unblocks the Dashboard and the per-check trend without a heavy dependency; lazy-loading contains its bundle cost.\n\n**Negative / watch**\n- **Scope expansion**: pulling Settings + Admin-reconcile into Week 6 grows the week beyond the original roadmap. Tracked honestly in `progress.md` (W6 task count rises; CLAUDE.md §13 headline updated).\n- New runtime dependency (recharts) enters the `pnpm audit` + Dependabot surface — acceptable, but it's one more thing to keep patched.\n- Removing the create/edit **drawers** touches `Connections.tsx` / `Suites.tsx` / the drawer components; must dedup with the open frontend nits ([#204](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/204)) and keep the extracted form specs (`connectionFormSpec.ts` / `checkForm.ts` / `suiteTarget.ts`) as the shared source for both create and edit.\n- Settings/Secrets/Admin surfaces touch sensitive config — hold the anti-lock-in line (no Azure names in logic) and the KPI-honesty line (no metric without a backing query).\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n- **`@ant-design/charts`** — visually on-brand out of the box, but G2Plot/G2 underneath makes it ~300 KB+ gz with a much larger dep/audit tree. Rejected: weight out' weighs the brand-fit gain for our 4–5 simple chart types.\n- **Apache ECharts** — most powerful, canvas-based; overkill and the largest footprint. Rejected for v1.\n- **Build the subset (just Dashboard + Results route) in Week 6, defer Profile/Settings/Admin to Week 7.** Rejected: that is exactly the under-scoping this ADR corrects — the screens are cohesive (shared shell, nav, error pages), and splitting them fragments the adoption.\n- **Keep create/edit as drawers, only add new pages.** Rejected: the prototype's structural decision is dedicated pages everywhere except Share; partial adoption leaves an inconsistent navigation model.\n"
}

277	> Adopt the **DataQ Design System** prototype (claude.ai/design project *"DataQ Design System"*, reverse-engineered from the shipped indigo/antd app — its `readme.md` is the spec). The **foundation already matches** the product (`theme.ts` palette, `resultsFormat.ts` status model, the 56px-header/220px-sider shell), so this is **not a re-skin** — it adopts the prototype's **structure**: every create/edit flow becomes a dedicated, deep-linkable **page** (the **Share** drawer stays the *only* drawer), login lands on an **Enhanced Monitoring Dashboard**, and standard **error pages** ship. Components are reproduced with **real antd** — the prototype's framework-free recreations + `_ds_bundle.js` are reference only (per the design README). 149:- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (ADLS / S3) — same stats via Pandas on sampled file — extended `profile_service` into a type dispatcher (`profile_connection`): SQL types → in-warehouse aggregation (unchanged), flat-file types (`adls_gen2`/`s3`) → download a **sample** of the file into Pandas and compute the same stats (`profile_dataframe`). Same `POST /suites/{id}/profile` endpoint, now polymorphic: SQL targets pass `table`/`schema`, flat-file targets pass `path` (+ optional `file_format`, else inferred from extension); response carries whichever identity applies. CSV + Parquet. **pandas "load less data" levers** (per the user's scale.html prompt): column projection (CSV `usecols`, Parquet `columns=` via pyarrow schema) so profiling 3 of N columns doesn't read all N, + row sampling (`_SAMPLE_ROWS`), + **zero-copy Arrow-backed dtypes** on the Parquet read (`dtype_backend="pyarrow"` — parquet is already Arrow, kept zero-copy; the stat helpers + `_to_native` are Arrow-scalar/`pd.NA`-safe). Stats are vectorised pandas (no Python row loops — enhancingperf N/A; CoW is a no-op for a read-only profiler + a process-global flag, so not toggled; pyarrow CSV engine rejected — it's incompatible with `nrows`/callable-`usecols`). NaN/Timestamp→JSON-safe. v1 → 422: unsupported type (UC still pending), missing target, unknown format, missing column; read failure → 502 (exception never echoed). Reuses `ColumnProfile`/`ProfileResult`. 24 new tests (pure `profile_dataframe`/`infer_file_format`/`_to_native`/projection-parse + endpoint via fake `_read_dataframe`); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 85% (only the live `_open_connection`/`_download_bytes` network seams uncovered — deferred smoke). _Deferred: streaming/range reads (whole object still downloaded before sampling) + out-of-core (Dask) — future work if profiling cost bites._

Components are reproduced with **real antd** — the prototype's framework-free recreations + `_ds_bundle.js` are reference only (per the design README). Backend-gated screens (Admin, Settings) + post-v1 items (marketing, dark mode) are tracked in Week 7 + Post-v1 below. The intentionally-dropped prototype bits (the **\"View as\" role switch**, the **component bundle**, the **teal palette**) are recorded in Post-v1 as *not adopted*.",
  "new_string": "### Prototype adoption — full screen set as dedicated-page flows (added — not in original roadmap) (10 tasks — 0/10) — tracked in epic [#175](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/175); ADR [0022](adr/0022-week6-prototype-adoption-and-chart-library.md)\n> Adopt the **DataQ Design System** prototype (claude.ai/design project *\"DataQ Design System\"*, reverse-engineered from the shipped indigo/antd app — its `readme.md` is the spec) — **the full 13-screen set this week**, not a subset (ADR [0022](adr/0022-week6-prototype-adoption-and-chart-library.md); the earlier 6-task scope under-counted it — Profile content, **Settings** (pulled in from Week 7), the Admin layout-reconcile, and the New-Connection source-select split were missing). The **foundation already matches** the product (`theme.ts` palette, `resultsFormat.ts` status model, the 56px-header/220px-sider shell), so this is **not a re-skin** — it adopts the prototype's **structure**: every create/edit flow becomes a dedicated, deep-linkable **page** (the **Share** drawer stays the *only* drawer), login lands on an **Enhanced Monitoring Dashboard**, and standard **error pages** ship. **Where the prototype differs from the shipped app (drawer-vs-page, layout), the prototype wins.** Components are reproduced with **real antd** — the prototype's framework-free recreations + `_ds_bundle.js` are reference only (per the design README). Post-v1 items (marketing, dark mode) + the polish passes (KPI honesty, visual-fidelity) stay in Week 7 / Post-v1 below. The intentionally-dropped prototype bits (the **\"View as\" role switch**, the **component bundle**, the **teal palette**) are recorded in Post-v1 as *not adopted*."
}

344	> Closes out the prototype after the Week-6 structural pass: the two screens that need backend (**Admin**, **Settings**) plus the final visual-fidelity pass to the design-system guidelines. Admin needs a real **workspace-admin** authz concept (today `admin` is per-suite only) — the prototype's client-side "View as" switch is **not** used (real authz is server-side, CLAUDE.md §11). **To open the prototype, see the *Design source* note in the Week-6 prototype-adoption group** (project `317fec67-2b8a-498c-8f6b-523750916a8d` via the `DesignSync` tool).
345	- [x] ✅ **Backend: workspace-admin list-all endpoint** — **shipped early in Week 4 (#289)** to unblock the Admin page. `GET /admin/suites` · `/admin/users` · `/admin/access` behind a **workspace-admin** authz check (config `WORKSPACE_ADMIN_EMAILS` allowlist, distinct from the per-suite `view < edit < admin < owner` ladder; no migration). Deliberately unscoped (bypasses owned-or-shared). `/me` exposes `is_workspace_admin`.
346	- [x] ✅ **Admin control centre page** (`/admin`) — **shipped early in Week 4 (#289)**. Suites / Users / Access tabs on the new endpoint, with a **real 403** `Forbidden` page for non-admins (server-driven via `/me`'s `is_workspace_admin` — *not* a client "View as" toggle), admin-only nav, shared `MeProvider`/`useMe`.
347	- [ ] ⬜ **Workspace Settings page** (`/settings`, `SettingsScreen`) — the app-shell settings screen. **No settings/preferences backend exists today** (only the `v1/` routers), so v1 ships the page shell + only controls that map to a real backend (e.g. Teams alert webhook config once the Week-6 `ResultPublisher` notification-config UI lands); everything else renders as a clearly-labelled placeholder until a settings API exists. Backend-gated. _Design ref: `templates/app/SettingsScreen.jsx`._
348	- [ ] ⬜ **Dashboard KPI honesty pass** — the Dashboard renders only metrics with a real data model: pass-rate, run counts, and `metric_value`-backed trends (ADR 0012 seam). **Omit "anomalies detected"** (the `anomaly` monitor-kind is reserved, not implemented in v1) and **"avg. time to resolution"** (there is no incident/resolution model). Re-introduce when the monitor kinds / model exist (post-v1 Theme A). _Design ref: `templates/app/DashboardScreen.jsx` (the KPI card row — keep pass-rate / runs / trends, drop the two unbacked KPIs)._
349	- [ ] ⬜ **Visual-fidelity pass to the design spec** — self-hosted **Inter** + **JetBrains Mono** `@font-face` (if not already wired), motion tokens (120–180ms ease on bg/border/shadow, 0.5px press nudge), the 2px indigo focus ring, hover elevation on interactive cards, and empty/loading states — matched to the prototype's `guidelines/*` foundation cards. Keep using **semantic tokens** so post-v1 dark mode inherits for free. _Design ref: `guidelines/{type-body,type-mono,color-indigo,color-neutrals,color-status,spacing-scale,elevation,radius}.card.html` + `tokens/{fonts,typography,elevation,spacing,colors}.css` + `assets/fonts/`; readme §"Visual foundations" / "Motion" / "Focus" / "Iconography"._

pass/fail per check, observed vs expected values, sample failing rows\n- [ ] Implement `get_health_score` resource — overall health score and 7-day trend, filterable by env and datasource type\n- [ ] Implement `get_adf_pipeline_status` resource — latest ADF pipeline run status with correlated DQ result per pipeline\n- [ ] Implement `trigger_suite_run` tool — async GX suite execution against a given datasource + env; returns `run_id` for polling\n- [ ] Implement `get_run_status` tool — poll live check-by-check progress for a running execution using `run_id`\n- [ ] Implement `create_check` tool — add a new GX expectation to a suite (form path: check type + column + threshold; or raw SQL)\n- [ ] Implement `profile_column` tool — run column profiler on a table or file; returns nulls, distinct count, min/max, top values\n- [ ] Write LLM-optimised docstrings for all 8 tools — describe what the tool does, when to use it, what parameters mean, and what the response contains; closes out the prototype after the Week-6 structural pass. ## Where we stand — the Week-6 build queue

The full screen set, in a sensible build order (each ships as its own commit per the working agreement):

1. **Foundation:** install recharts; **nav/shell restructure** (add Dashboard, footer group, `/`→`/dashboard`) — unblocks everything
2. **`/results/:runId` route** (drawer → page + CSV/JSON download) — the recent-runs drill-down target
3. **`/dashboard`** landing — KPI cards, Quality Trends, Suite Performance bars, recent-runs table
4. **Drawer → page restructures:** New Suite, then edit flows (connection/suite/check), New-Connection source-select split
5. **Profile content**, **Settings**, **Admin reconcile**
6. **Error pages** (4xx/5xx)
7. **Alerting track** (backend): `ResultPublisher` seam → Teams config → suppression/dedup/routing

3	/**
4	 * App-wide Ant Design theme. Kept in one module so the brand palette and the
5	 * shell (App.tsx) read from the same tokens rather than hard-coding hexes.

7	 * Design intent: move off the stock antd "navy-header admin template" look.
8	 * An indigo primary + a soft gray layout canvas (`colorBgLayout`) give the
9	 * white surfaces (header, sider, cards) depth and definition — so the page no
10	 * longer reads as a flat sea of white, and the empty space frames content
11	 * instead of just being blank.
12	 */

15	  /** Indigo-600 — the primary accent (buttons, active nav, links). */

20	  selectedBg: '#eef0fe',

1	import type { ResultStatus, RunStatus } from '../../api/runs';
2	
3	/**
4	 * Pure presentation helpers for the Results surface — kept framework-free so the
5	 * status→colour mapping and the timing formatters can be unit-tested without
6	 * rendering antd.
7	 */
8	
9	/** antd Tag colour per run execution status. */
10	export const RUN_STATUS_COLORS: Record<RunStatus, string> = {
11	  queued: 'default',
12	  running: 'processing',
13	  succeeded: 'success',
14	  failed: 'error',
15	  cancelled: 'warning',
16	};
17	
18	/**
19	 * antd `Progress` bar status per run lifecycle state. A `Record` (not a switch)
20	 * so a new `RunStatus` value is a compile error here rather than silently
21	 * falling through to `'normal'`.
22	 */
23	export const RUN_BAR_STATUS: Record<RunStatus, 'success' | 'exception' | 'active' | 'normal'> = {
24	  queued: 'normal',
25	  running: 'active',
26	  succeeded: 'success',
27	  failed: 'exception',
28	  cancelled: 'exception',
29	};
30	
31	/** antd Tag colour per result severity / operational status (ADR 0005 / #122). */
32	export const RESULT_STATUS_COLORS: Record<ResultStatus, string> = {
33	  pass: 'success',
34	  warn: 'warning',
35	  fail: 'error',
36	  critical: 'magenta',
37	  skip: 'default',
38	  error: 'volcano',
39	};
40	
41	/** Orchestrator pipeline-run status → colour (provider-agnostic value set). */
42	export function pipelineStatusColor(status: string): string {
43	  switch (status) {
44	    case 'succeeded':
45	      return 'success';
46	    case 'failed':
47	      return 'error';
48	    case 'running':
49	      return 'processing';
50	    case 'cancelled':
51	      return 'warning';
52	    default:
53	      return 'default';
54	  }
55	}
56	
57	/**
58	 * Render an unknown scalar (a GX observed/expected value, a profiled min/max or
59	 * top value) for display: an em dash for null/undefined, JSON for objects, the
60	 * `String` form otherwise. Falsy scalars (`0`, `false`, `''`) render as
61	 * themselves — not collapsed to the em dash — so a real zero isn't mistaken for
62	 * "no value". The em-dash sentinel matches `formatTimestamp` / `formatDuration`.
63	 */
64	export function formatScalar(value: unknown): string {
65	  if (value === null || value === undefined) return '—';
66	  return typeof value === 'object' ? JSON.stringify(value) : String(value);
67	}
68	
69	/** Absolute timestamp as a locale string, or an em dash when absent. */
70	export function formatTimestamp(iso: string | null): string {
71	  if (!iso) return '—';
72	  const d = new Date(iso);
73	  return Number.isNaN(d.getTime()) ? '—' : d.toLocaleString();
74	}
75	
76	/**
77	 * Run duration (finished − started) as a compact human string: `850ms`, `12s`,
78	 * `1m 3s`. Returns an em dash when either bound is missing (queued / never
79	 * finished) or the interval is negative (clock skew).
80	 */
81	export function formatDuration(startedAt: string | null, finishedAt: string | null): string {
82	  if (!startedAt || !finishedAt) return '—';
83	  const ms = new Date(finishedAt).getTime() - new Date(startedAt).getTime();
84	  if (Number.isNaN(ms) || ms < 0) return '—';
85	  if (ms < 1000) return `${ms}ms`;
86	  const totalSeconds = Math.round(ms / 1000);
87	  if (totalSeconds < 60) return `${totalSeconds}s`;
88	  const minutes = Math.floor(totalSeconds / 60);
89	  const seconds = totalSeconds % 60;
90	  return `${minutes}m ${seconds}s`;
91	}
92	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/charts/chartTheme.ts",
  "content": "import type { ResultStatus, RunStatus } from '../../api/runs';\nimport { BRAND } from '../../theme';\n\n/**\n * Chart colour tokens + shared axis/grid styling for the recharts-based\n * dashboard widgets (ADR 0022). Kept framework-free and separate from\n * `resultsFormat.ts` for one reason: that module maps statuses to **antd Tag\n * names** (`'success'`, `'magenta'`, …) which only antd understands, whereas\n * SVG charts need concrete hex. These maps are the chart-domain mirror of the\n * same severity/run-status semantics (readme status model · ADR 0005/0016) —\n * the hexes track antd v6's default palette so a chart series reads the same\n * colour as the matching Tag.\n *\n * `Record<…>` (not a lookup with a fallback) so adding a `ResultStatus` /\n * `RunStatus` value is a compile error here rather than a silently grey series.\n */\n\n/** Severity → series hex (pass green · warn gold · fail red · critical magenta). */\nexport const RESULT_STATUS_CHART_COLORS: Record<ResultStatus, string> = {\n  pass: '#52c41a', // green-6\n  warn: '#faad14', // gold-6\n  fail: '#ff4d4f', // red-6\n  critical: '#eb2f96', // magenta-6\n  skip: '#bfbfbf', // gray-5\n  error: '#fa541c', // volcano-6\n};\n\n/** Run execution status → series hex (succeeded green · failed red · running indigo · …). */\nexport const RUN_STATUS_CHART_COLORS: Record<RunStatus, string> = {\n  queued: '#bfbfbf', // gray-5\n  running: BRAND.primary, // indigo — matches the brand \"in-flight\" accent\n  succeeded: '#52c41a', // green-6\n  failed: '#ff4d4f', // red-6\n  cancelled: '#faad14', // gold-6\n};\n\n/**\n * Non-status chart tokens — the indigo primary for neutral/aggregate series\n * (e.g. total runs), plus the hairline grid + muted axis tints so every chart\n * frames the same way the cards/tables do.\n */\nexport const CHART_COLORS = {\n  primary: BRAND.primary,\n  grid: BRAND.border,\n  axis: '#8c8c8c',\n} as const;\n\n/** Shared recharts style props so axes/grid/tooltip are consistent across widgets. */\nexport const AXIS_TICK = { fontSize: 12, fill: CHART_COLORS.axis } as const;\nexport const GRID_PROPS = { stroke: CHART_COLORS.grid, strokeDasharray: '3 3' } as const;\nexport const TOOLTIP_STYLE = {\n  borderRadius: 8,\n  border: `1px solid ${BRAND.border}`,\n  fontSize: 13,\n} as const;\n\n/** Series colour for a result severity. */\nexport function severityColor(status: ResultStatus): string {\n  return RESULT_STATUS_CHART_COLORS[status];\n}\n\n/** Series colour for a run execution status. */\nexport function runStatusColor(status: RunStatus): string {\n  return RUN_STATUS_CHART_COLORS[status];\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/charts/ResponsiveChart.tsx",
  "content": "import { Empty } from 'antd';\nimport type { ReactElement } from 'react';\nimport { ResponsiveContainer } from 'recharts';\n\n/**\n * Thin wrapper every dashboard chart mounts through, so they share one height /\n * width contract and one empty state. recharts is only ever imported from here\n * (and the chart widgets that use it), and the chart widgets live on lazy routes\n * (`/dashboard`, run/check detail) — so recharts stays out of the initial bundle\n * and only ships when a chart route loads (ADR 0022).\n *\n * `ResponsiveContainer` needs exactly one child element — pass a single recharts\n * chart (`<BarChart>`, `<LineChart>`, …).\n */\ninterface ResponsiveChartProps {\n  /** A single recharts chart element. */\n  children: ReactElement;\n  height?: number;\n  /** Render the empty state instead of the chart (no data for the range). */\n  isEmpty?: boolean;\n  emptyText?: string;\n}\n\nexport function ResponsiveChart({\n  children,\n  height = 240,\n  isEmpty = false,\n  emptyText = 'No data for this range yet',\n}: ResponsiveChartProps) {\n  if (isEmpty) {\n    return (\n      <Empty\n        image={Empty.PRESENTED_IMAGE_SIMPLE}\n        description={emptyText}\n        style={{\n          height,\n          margin: 0,\n          display: 'flex',\n          flexDirection: 'column',\n          alignItems: 'center',\n          justifyContent: 'center',\n        }}\n      />\n    );\n  }\n  return (\n    <ResponsiveContainer width=\"100%\" height={height}>\n      {children}\n    </ResponsiveContainer>\n  );\n}\n"
}

=== runs router head ===
"""Run / result / pipeline-run read API — the results surface (PR-C0b).

Read-only views over `runs`, `results`, and `pipeline_runs` for the in-app
results page. The DQ-run reads (`/runs`, `/runs/{id}`) are **suite-scoped**: the
list filters to suites the caller can access and the detail gates on
`require_permission(view)`, so per-suite sharing + existence-hiding hold exactly
as for suites/checks (this is why reading Postgres directly — e.g. a Grafana
panel — is rejected as the primary surface: it bypasses this authz + the sample
redaction below). `/pipeline_runs` is orchestration monitoring, not suite-scoped,
so it is gated on authentication only.

Manual run *triggering* lives on `POST /suites/{id}/run` in `suites.py` (the
suite-scoped, edit-gated write); this module owns the reads.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import uuid
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Annotated, Any

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Query, status
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user
from backend.app.db.models import User
from backend.app.db.session import get_db
from backend.app.services import orchestration_service, run_dispatch
from backend.app.services import run_service as svc
from backend.app.services.suite_authz import require_permission

router = APIRouter(tags=["runs"])

_LIST_LIMIT_DEFAULT = 50
_LIST_LIMIT_MAX = 200


class RunRead(BaseModel):
    """A DQ suite run (execution lifecycle; `status` is execution, not pass/fail)."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)

    id: uuid.UUID
    suite_id: uuid.UUID
    status: str  # queued | running | succeeded | failed | cancelled
    triggered_by: str | None
    started_at: datetime | None
    finished_at: datetime | None
    created_at: datetime


class ResultRead(BaseModel):
    """One check's result within a run. `metric_value` is the SQL-aggregatable
    badness scalar (ADR 0012); `observed_value`/`expected_value` are GX summary
    values (same fields the dry-run / probe already surface).

    `sample_failures` (the raw failing rows) is **deliberately not exposed** here:
    it can carry real data and is treated as redactor-only elsewhere (gx_runner,
    and the dry-run path omits it for the same reason). Surfacing it on the
    results drill-down needs row-level redaction / an opt-in policy first — see
    the follow-up issue, to land with the Results page (PR-C1)."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)

    id: uuid.UUID
    check_id: uuid.UUID
    status: str  # pass | warn | fail | critical | skip | error
    metric_value: float | None
    duration_ms: int | None
```

=== runs.py rest (list + scoping) ===
    duration_ms: int | None
    observed_value: dict[str, Any] | None
    expected_value: dict[str, Any] | None


class RunDetailRead(RunRead):
    results: list[ResultRead]


class CheckProgressRead(BaseModel):
    """One check's progress; `status` is null while the check is still pending."""

    check_id: uuid.UUID
    name: str
    status: str | None  # null = pending | pass | warn | fail | critical | skip | error


class RunProgressRead(BaseModel):
    """Compact live-progress view for polling: run lifecycle + per-check
    resolution + a status histogram. Lighter than the full run+results detail."""

    run_id: uuid.UUID
    suite_id: uuid.UUID
    status: str  # queued | running | succeeded | failed | cancelled
    total_checks: int
    completed_checks: int
    counts: dict[str, int]  # histogram over result statuses (all keys present)
    checks: list[CheckProgressRead]
    started_at: datetime | None
    finished_at: datetime | None


class PipelineRunRead(BaseModel):
    """A monitored orchestrator pipeline/DAG run (`pipeline_runs` ≠ `runs`)."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)

    id: uuid.UUID
    provider: str  # adf | airflow
    connection_id: uuid.UUID
    provider_run_id: str
    pipeline_or_dag_id: str
    env: str
    status: str
    started_at: datetime | None
    finished_at: datetime | None
    failure_reason: str | None
    created_at: datetime


@router.get("/runs", response_model=list[RunRead], summary="List runs")
def list_runs(
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,
    run_status: Annotated[str | None, Query(alias="status")] = None,
    limit: int = Query(default=_LIST_LIMIT_DEFAULT, ge=1, le=_LIST_LIMIT_MAX),
) -> list[RunRead]:
    # When a suite is named, gate on it up front so an inaccessible/unknown suite
    # 404s (existence hidden) rather than silently returning []. With no suite,
    # the service scopes to every suite the caller can access.
    if suite_id is not None:
        require_permission(db, suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="view")
    runs = svc.list_runs(
        db, user_id=current_user.id, suite_id=suite_id, status=run_status, limit=limit
    )
    return [RunRead.model_validate(r) for r in runs]


@router.get("/runs/{run_id}", response_model=RunDetailRead, summary="Get a run with its results")
def get_run(
    run_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> RunDetailRead:
    run = svc.get_run(db, run_id)
    if run is None:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
    # Gate on the run's suite: a caller who can't see the suite can't see its
    # runs (404 hides the run id too, matching the suite existence-hiding rule).
    require_permission(db, run.suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="view")
    results = svc.list_results(db, run_id)
    # `Run` has no `results` relationship to validate a RunDetailRead from
    # directly, so validate the run fields (as RunRead) and graft the
    # separately-fetched, redaction-gated results on.
    return RunDetailRead(
        **RunRead.model_validate(run).model_dump(),
        results=[ResultRead.model_validate(r) for r in results],
    )


@router.get(
    "/runs/{run_id}/progress",
    response_model=RunProgressRead,
    summary="Poll a run's live progress (lifecycle + per-check status)",
)
def get_run_progress(
    run_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> RunProgressRead:
    run = svc.get_run(db, run_id)
    if run is None:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
    # Same suite-scoped gate as the run detail: can't see the suite → 404 the run.
    require_permission(db, run.suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="view")
    progress = svc.get_run_progress(db, run)
    return RunProgressRead(
        run_id=run.id,
        suite_id=run.suite_id,
        status=run.status,
        total_checks=progress.total_checks,
        completed_checks=progress.completed_checks,
        counts=progress.counts,
        checks=[
            CheckProgressRead(check_id=c.check_id, name=c.name, status=c.status)
            for c in progress.checks
        ],
        started_at=run.started_at,
        finished_at=run.finished_at,
    )


@router.post(
    "/runs/{run_id}/cancel",
    response_model=RunRead,
    summary="Cancel a queued or running run",
)
def cancel_run(
    run_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> RunRead:
    """Cancel a non-terminal run. `edit`-gated (same capability as triggering).

    Marks the run `cancelled` and best-effort revokes its Celery task (dropping it
    if still queued). An already-finished run (succeeded/failed/cancelled) → 409.
    An in-flight run is stopped cooperatively by the worker (it won't overwrite a
    `cancelled` status with results), so cancel may race a fast run to completion.
    """
    run = svc.get_run(db, run_id)
    if run is None:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
    # Cancel is a control action on the suite's runs → edit (404 hides the run
    # for a caller who can't see the suite, matching the read endpoints).
    require_permission(db, run.suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="edit")
    if not svc.cancel_run(db, run):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="run is already finished")
    run_dispatch.revoke_run(run.celery_task_id)
    return RunRead.model_validate(run)


@router.get(
    "/pipeline_runs",
    response_model=list[PipelineRunRead],
    summary="List monitored orchestrator pipeline/DAG runs",
)
def list_pipeline_runs(
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
    provider: str | None = None,
```

210	# ── read model (PR-C0b: the runs/results surface) ────────────────────────────
211	# Reads are scoped to suites the user can access — owned (`created_by`) or shared
212	# (`shares`), the same visibility `suite_service.list_suites` enforces. The API
213	# layer additionally calls `require_permission` for single-suite / single-run
214	# lookups (404 hides existence); this subquery is the defence-in-depth filter so
215	# a list query can never leak a run from a suite the caller can't see.
216	
217	
218	def list_runs(
219	    session: Session,
220	    *,
221	    user_id: uuid.UUID,
222	    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,
223	    status: str | None = None,
224	    limit: int = 50,
225	) -> list[Run]:
226	    """Runs for suites the user can access, newest first (`created_at` desc).
227	
228	    Optionally narrowed to one ``suite_id`` and/or a ``status``. The accessible
229	    subquery is always applied, so passing a ``suite_id`` the user can't see
230	    yields an empty list (the API layer 404s that case up front via
231	    `require_permission`, but the filter keeps the service safe on its own).
232	    """
233	    accessible = suite_service.accessible_suite_ids(user_id)
234	    stmt = (
235	        select(Run).where(Run.suite_id.in_(accessible)).order_by(Run.created_at.desc()).limit(limit)
236	    )
237	    if suite_id is not None:
238	        stmt = stmt.where(Run.suite_id == suite_id)
239	    if status is not None:
240	        stmt = stmt.where(Run.status == status)
241	    return list(session.scalars(stmt))
242	
243	
244	def get_run(session: Session, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> Run | None:
245	    """Fetch a run by id (no authz — the API layer gates on the run's suite)."""
246	    return session.get(Run, run_id)
247	
248	
249	_TERMINAL_STATUSES = frozenset({"succeeded", "failed", "cancelled"})
250	
251	
252	def cancel_run(session: Session, run: Run) -> bool:
253	    """Transition a non-terminal run to ``cancelled``; return whether it changed.
254	
255	    Returns ``False`` if the run is already terminal (succeeded/failed/cancelled)
256	    — the API surfaces that as 409. Sets ``finished_at``; ``started_at`` is left
257	    as-is (NULL if the run was still queued). This is the DB half; the API layer
258	    also best-effort revokes the Celery task, and the worker honours the
259	    ``cancelled`` status cooperatively (start-check + in-flight guard).
260	    """
261	    if run.status in _TERMINAL_STATUSES:
262	        return False
263	    run.status = "cancelled"
264	    run.finished_at = _now()
265	    session.commit()
266	    log.info("run_cancelled", run_id=str(run.id))
267	    return True
268	
269	
270	def list_results(session: Session, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[Result]:
271	    """The result rows for a run, in stable check order (`created_at`)."""
272	    return list(
273	        session.scalars(select(Result).where(Result.run_id == run_id).order_by(Result.created_at))
274	    )

=== severity.py ===
"""Severity post-processing — derive a result's tier from check thresholds.

The single home for the ADR-0005 tier derivation + the ADR-0012 `metric_value`
extraction, kept pure (no DB, no GX) so the semantics live in one place: if we
ever switch from banding the *unexpected fraction* to banding the *raw observed
value* (ADR 0016 approach B), only this module changes.

Model (ADR 0016, approach A):
- `metric_value` = the GX **unexpected percent** (0-100): how badly the rule was
  violated. 0 = clean, higher = worse. It is the SQL-aggregatable badness scalar
  (ADR 0012) and the quantity the thresholds band.
- When a check has thresholds, they are the user's severity policy and they
  fully determine the tier — overriding GX's binary success (e.g. a check with
  `mostly=0.99` that GX fails at 0.5% unexpected resolves to `pass` if the user's
  `warn` threshold is 1%). The stored `metric_value` keeps this transparent.
- No thresholds set, or no metric to band (aggregate checks like row_count that
  produce no unexpected fraction) → binary `pass`/`fail` from GX success
  (ADR 0005 binary fallback).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation
from typing import Any

from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome

# GX key carrying the violated-row fraction (0-100), copied into sample_failures
# by the runner. This is the badness scalar the thresholds band.
_UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY = "unexpected_percent"


def extract_metric(outcome: CheckOutcome) -> Decimal | None:
    """The numeric badness scalar for a check, or None if it has none.

    Reads the GX unexpected-percent from the outcome's sample detail. Computed at
    run time and persisted to `results.metric_value`, so it survives the later
    sample-failures retention purge (the durable scalar the dashboard trends).
    Uses ``Decimal(str(...))`` so a float like ``0.5`` lands as exact ``0.5`` in
    the NUMERIC column rather than its binary expansion.
    """
    sample: dict[str, Any] | None = outcome.sample_failures
    if not sample or _UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY not in sample:
        return None
    raw = sample[_UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY]
    if raw is None:
        return None
    try:
        metric = Decimal(str(raw))
    except (InvalidOperation, ValueError, TypeError):
        return None
    # GX can emit NaN/inf — e.g. unexpected_percent on an empty table is 0/0
    # (the same reason run_service runs a NaN->null sanitizer). A non-finite
    # Decimal slips past the parse but would make every threshold comparison
    # False (silently 'pass') or raise mid-run; treat it as "no bandable
    # metric" so derive_status falls back to binary pass/fail.
    return metric if metric.is_finite() else None


def resolve_status(
    outcome: CheckOutcome,
    *,
    warn_threshold: Decimal | None,
    fail_threshold: Decimal | None,
    critical_threshold: Decimal | None,
) -> tuple[str, Decimal | None]:
    """Resolve a check outcome to its persisted ``(status, metric_value)``.

    The single decision both run-result persistence (`run_service`) and the
    check-editor dry-run (`dryrun_service`) share, so a preview can never disagree
    with the run it previews:

    * a check the runner could not *evaluate* (`outcome.errored`, #122) is the
      operational ``error`` status — no severity tier, no metric to band; vs.
    * an evaluated check, whose unexpected-% metric is banded into a tier
      (ADR 0005 / 0016).
    """
    if outcome.errored:
        return "error", None
    metric = extract_metric(outcome)
    status = derive_status(
        success=outcome.success,
        metric_value=metric,
        warn_threshold=warn_threshold,
        fail_threshold=fail_threshold,
        critical_threshold=critical_threshold,
    )
    return status, metric


def derive_status(
    *,
    success: bool,
    metric_value: Decimal | None,
    warn_threshold: Decimal | None,
    fail_threshold: Decimal | None,
    critical_threshold: Decimal | None,
) -> str:
    """Resolve a result's tier (`pass`/`warn`/`fail`/`critical`), ADR 0005.

    Higher `metric_value` is worse; thresholds are ordered (warn ≤ fail ≤
    critical) and any unset threshold is skipped (treated as +∞). Falls back to
    binary pass/fail when the check carries no thresholds, or carries thresholds
    but produced no bandable metric.
    """
    no_thresholds = warn_threshold is None and fail_threshold is None and critical_threshold is None
    if no_thresholds or metric_value is None:
        return "pass" if success else "fail"

    if critical_threshold is not None and metric_value >= critical_threshold:
        return "critical"
    if fail_threshold is not None and metric_value >= fail_threshold:
        return "fail"
    if warn_threshold is not None and metric_value >= warn_threshold:
        return "warn"
    return "pass"

id: toolu_015yk9ggG9ZADUGcuRFbYQjd
```
1	# ADR 0005 — Severity tier weights (warn / fail / critical → health score)
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-05-30
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	v1 checks are not binary pass/fail. A check can carry up to three optional
10	thresholds (`warn_threshold`, `fail_threshold`, `critical_threshold`), and a run
11	result lands in one of four states: `pass`, `warn`, `fail`, `critical`. The
12	dashboard (Week 6) rolls these per-result states up into a single **health
13	score** stat card + 7-day trend, and alert routing (Week 6) keys off the same
14	tiers (`warn` quiet, `fail` standard, `critical` @channel).
15	
16	This decision must land **before the Week-3 threshold migration** because two
17	things bake into schema and stored data:
18	
19	1. The `status` value set (`pass | warn | fail | critical`) becomes a CHECK
20	   constraint on `results`.
21	2. The **weights** that map a tier to a health-score penalty determine how
22	   historical results aggregate. Once results are written and trends are drawn,
23	   re-weighting silently rewrites the meaning of every past data point. The
24	   roadmap calls this out explicitly: *"This affects the DB schema for run
25	   results and cannot be changed cheaply after data is written."*
26	
27	The open question this ADR closes: **what are the weights, and what is the
28	health-score formula** — so the Week-6 dashboard and the Week-8 result-service
29	tests have a fixed target, and the Week-3 migration writes the right CHECK.
30	
31	## Decision
32	
33	**Adopt four ordered severity tiers with fixed penalty weights, and a single
34	normalised health-score formula that is SQL-aggregatable.**
35	
36	### Tiers and weights
37	
38	| Status | Penalty weight | Meaning | Alert behaviour (Week 6) |
39	|---|---|---|---|
40	| `pass` | `0.0` | check met its expectation | none |
41	| `warn` | `0.5` | soft threshold breached — flag only | quiet (no @mention) |
42	| `fail` | `1.0` | check failed | standard alert |
43	| `critical` | `2.0` | failed past the critical threshold | urgent (@channel) |
44	
45	Weights are **not** stored per-result. Only the `status` string is persisted;
46	the weight is a fixed lookup applied at aggregation time. This keeps the weights
47	re-tunable in code *for forward-looking display* without a data migration — but
48	the **tier a result was assigned** is immutable history (it is what the
49	thresholds evaluated to at run time).
50	
51	### Health-score formula
52	
53	For any set of N check results in scope (a suite, an env, a time window):
54	
55	```
56	penalty(status) = {pass:0.0, warn:0.5, fail:1.0, critical:2.0}
57	
58	health_score = 100 × (1 − Σ penalty(statusᵢ) / (N × W_max))   where W_max = 2.0
59	```
60	
61	- Range is a clean `[0, 100]`: all-`pass` → 100, all-`critical` → 0,
62	  all-`fail` → 50, all-`warn` → 75.
63	- `W_max = 2.0` (the `critical` weight) is the normaliser, so a suite that is
64	  entirely `fail` still scores above the floor — `critical` is meaningfully
65	  worse than `fail`, not collapsed to the same 0.
66	- It is a pure `SUM(...)/COUNT(*)` over the persisted `status` column → computes
67	  in one SQL pass for any filter (env / datasource / suite / date range), no
68	  per-check application code. This is the same aggregation discipline as the
69	  `metric_value` numeric column (ADR 0012): the dashboard never reduces JSONB
70	  in Python.
71	
72	### Binary fallback
73	
74	A check with no tier thresholds set behaves as plain pass/fail: it resolves to
75	`pass` (penalty 0) or `fail` (penalty 1.0). `warn` and `critical` are simply
76	never assigned. The formula is unchanged — binary checks are the N-tier formula
77	with two of four tiers unused.
78	
79	## Consequences
80	
81	**Positive**
82	- Week-3 migration writes a settled `status` CHECK (`pass | warn | fail |
83	  critical`); no re-migration when the dashboard lands.
84	- Week-6 health score and Week-8 result-service tests have an exact, testable
85	  target (e.g. `{fail, fail, pass, pass}` → 75.0).
86	- Weights live in one constant, aggregation is one SQL expression — severity-aware
87	  alert routing reads the same `status` column.
88	
89	**Negative**
90	- Weights (0.5 / 1.0 / 2.0) are a judgement call, not derived from data. Accepted:
91	  they are display-time weights, re-tunable in code without touching stored
92	  history; only the *tier assignment* is immutable.
93	- A single global health score can mask a single catastrophic table inside an
94	  otherwise-green suite. Mitigated by the per-suite / per-check breakdowns the
95	  dashboard renders alongside the headline number (Week 6).
96	
97	## Alternatives considered
98	
99	- **Normalise by `W_max = 1.0` (the `fail` weight), clamp at 0.** All-`fail` → 0,
100	  all-`critical` → 0 (clamped). Rejected: collapses the fail/critical distinction
101	  at the aggregate floor, which is exactly the signal critical exists to carry.
102	- **Store the weight on each result row.** Rejected: bloats `results`, and makes
103	  a weight change a data migration instead of a constant edit, while gaining
104	  nothing the status string + lookup doesn't already give.
105	- **Binary pass/fail only for v1, tiers later.** Rejected: tiers are a Week-3
106	  roadmap deliverable and the threshold columns ride the one-shot Week-3 schema
107	  migration (ADR 0012). Adding `warn`/`critical` to the `status` CHECK later is a
108	  second backward-compat two-step — the precise retrofit this ADR exists to avoid.
109	- **Continuous severity (0.0–1.0 float per result) instead of four tiers.**
110	  Rejected for v1: no UI affordance to author a continuous severity, and alert
111	  routing needs discrete buckets anyway. The numeric `metric_value` (ADR 0012)
112	  already preserves the underlying scalar for anyone who wants finer analysis.
113	
114	## Related
115	
116	- ADR 0012 — monitor-kind seam (`check.kind` + `metric_value` / `duration_ms`);
117	  **rides the same Week-3 migration** as the threshold/status columns.
118	- `docs/progress.md` — Week 3 "Severity threshold tiers" tasks; Week 6 health
119	  score + severity-aware alert routing; Week 8 result-service tests.
120	- `context/DataQ_platform_roadmap.md` — Week 3 severity tiers; post-v1 Theme A
121	  (auto-monitors emit the same tiers over `metric_value`).
122	- CLAUDE.md §9 (decision table), §5 (monitor-kind seam).
123	
```

```
"""Tests for the run/result persistence service.

No database or GX: a fake Session records what would be persisted, model
instances are built in memory with explicit ids, and a fake CheckRunner returns
canned outcomes (or raises). This keeps the service's lifecycle + mapping logic
under test independent of Postgres and Snowflake.
"""

import uuid
from decimal import Decimal

from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
from backend.app.db.models import Check, Result, Run
from backend.app.services import run_service


class FakeSession:
    """Records add_all'd rows; counts commits/rollbacks. `add_all_raises` simulates
    a persistence failure (e.g. DB error) after the adapter has already run."""

    def __init__(
        self, *, add_all_raises: Exception | None = None, refresh_status: str | None = None
    ) -> None:
        self.added: list[Result] = []
        self.commits = 0
        self.rollbacks = 0
        self._add_all_raises = add_all_raises
        # When set, refresh() stamps this onto the refreshed object's `status`,
        # simulating a concurrent cancel that committed from another session.
        self._refresh_status = refresh_status

    def add_all(self, rows: list[Result]) -> None:
        if self._add_all_raises is not None:
            raise self._add_all_raises
        self.added.extend(rows)

    def commit(self) -> None:
        self.commits += 1

    def rollback(self) -> None:
        self.rollbacks += 1
        self.added.clear()  # discard staged-but-uncommitted rows, like a real rollback

    def refresh(self, obj: object) -> None:
        if self._refresh_status is not None:
            obj.status = self._refresh_status  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


class FakeRunner:
    def __init__(
        self, outcome: SuiteOutcome | None = None, raises: Exception | None = None
    ) -> None:
        self._outcome = outcome
        self._raises = raises
        self.called_with: dict[str, object] | None = None

    def run_checks(
        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]
    ) -> SuiteOutcome:
        self.called_with = {"table": table, "schema": schema, "checks": checks}
        if self._raises is not None:
            raise self._raises
        assert self._outcome is not None
        return self._outcome


def _run() -> Run:
    return Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), status="queued")


=== how is TEST_DATABASE_URL set in CI? ===

.github/workflows/ci.yml:177:          TEST_DATABASE_URL: REDACTED
```
